Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Primitives only.\n156: //\n157: // RawBody is a *string so GET can omit the field (nil) while POST with an\n158: // empty body still emits \"raw_body\":\"\" (non-nil pointer to \"\"). A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Kept as a guard for\n209: \t\t// direct invocation in tests.\n210: \t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n211: \t\treturn\n212: \t}\n213: }\n214: \n215: // Routes registers the mirror domain routes onto the mux.\n216: func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux) {\n217: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n218: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"POST /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n219: }\n220: \n221: // mirrorHeaders returns a copy of the header map with redacted headers removed.\n222: func mirrorHeaders(h http.Header) map[string][]string {\n223: \tout := make(map[string][]string, len(h))\n224: \tfor k, v := range h {\n225: \t\tif redactedHeaders[k] {\n226: \t\t\tcontinue\n227: \t\t}\n228: \t\tout[k] = v\n229: \t}\n230: \treturn out\n231: }\n232: ```\n233: \n234: 3. Imports are: `encoding/base64`, `io`, `log/slog`, `net/http`, and `github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web`.\n235: 4. Use a `switch` statement (not if/else ladders) per `branching-logic-flow` skill.\n236: 5. `RawBody` is `*string` with `omitempty`: nil for GET (field absent), non-nil for POST\n237:    (field always present, `\"\"` for an empty body). Take the pointer with `new(expr)` — do not\n238:    add a `ptr` helper.\n239: \n240: ### W2: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp_test.go`\n241: \n242: | Field | Value |\n243: |-------|-------|\n244: | wave | 1 |\n245: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp_test.go` |\n246: | reads | `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp_test.go` (for test patterns: httptest.NewRequest, httptest.NewRecorder, mux.ServeHTTP) |\n247: | depends on | W1 (mirrorapp must exist, may not be compiled yet) |\n248: | done when | `go test ./app/domain/mirrorapp/ -v` passes all cases |\n249: \n250: **do:**\n251: \n252: 1. Ask concise follow-up questions on anything relevant.\n- Clarify options with the user, incorporate answers, then proceed to the next question or phase.\n- After the user answers, verify everything was covered or flag what remains.\n- Plan a change with the user first; proceed only once cleared. \"Cleared\" means a written\n  `.plans/<slug>/plan.md` the user has explicitly approved — answering your clarifying\n  questions is **not** approval, and neither is silence. If unsure, ask \"shall I write the plan?\"\n- Only a genuinely small change — a typo, a rename, a one-line fix in a file you have already\n  read — may skip the plan. A new route, type, package, or dependency never qualifies.\n- Never reference other plan files in the project unless the user explicitly does.\n\n## Git\n\n- You may stage, commit, and push to feature branches, and open or update PRs and issues.\n- Never merge, never push to the main branch, never force-push, and never delete branches or tags. If asked, refuse and give the user the commands to run.\n- Do not add a `Co-Authored-By` or any other trailer to commits.\n\n## Feature development — mandatory skills\n\nAlways apply these skills when doing feature work; do not rely on memory:\n\n- `project-profile` — first, if `.agents/project-profile.md` is missing. Also whenever another\n  skill reports the profile absent or stale.\n- `plan-change` — **first**, at the start of a development session and before any request to\n  add, change, modify, extend, refactor, or remove source code, in any language. Adding a route,\n  type, package, endpoint, or domain always requires it. Read-only on source: maps the request\n  onto the existing code and writes/revises `.plans/<slug>/plan.md` (color-coded Mermaid diagrams)\n  plus `.plans/<slug>/agent_instructions.md` (the subagent-ready work-unit brief), looping with\n  the user until approved. Do not write code until the plan is approved. Skip only for a trivial\n  single-line edit the user asked for directly.\n- `use-modern-go` — whenever writing or editing any Go code.\n- `branching-logic-flow` — when writing/refactoring conditional or branching logic in Go (default-first assignment, naked switches over if/else ladders).\n- `layered-architecture-types` — before writing, editing, or auditing any `app/*`, `business/domain/*`, or `.../stores/*db` Go file. Holds the App ↔ Business ↔ Storage type-boundary rules below.\n- `business-layer-extensions` — before adding a cross-cutting concern (OTEL, logging, metrics, caching, auth), creating files under `business/domain/*/extensions/*`, or adding the `ExtBusiness`/`Extension` seam to a `*bus` package.\n\n## Reviewing a PR\n\n- `pr-review` — for any PR / diff / pre-commit review. It runs a guided review: asks for missing inputs (diff scope, issue-tracker URL, issue id), reviews the diff against the ticket's acceptance criteria, and writes auto-numbered findings and Mermaid diagrams under `.reviews/<issue-id>/`.\n\n### Layer conversions (from `layered-architecture-types`)\n\nPrimitives live at the edges (API JSON structs, DB row structs); strong types (`business/types/*`) live only in the Business layer. Every crossing goes through a named converter — never assign across a boundary directly:\n\n- App → Business: `toBus<Type>` (parses + validates, returns `errs.FieldErrors`)\n- Business → App: `fromBus<Type>Response` (strong → primitive, explicit)\n- Business → Storage: `toDB<Type>`\n- Storage → Business: `toBus<Type>` (native → strong, returns error)\n\n## Editing order\n\nEdit so the tree is never gratuitously broken between tool calls:\n\n- Write a type before the code that references it — `model.go` before the handler that uses it.\n- Change an import and its call site in the **same** edit; never add an unused import and fix it later.\n- Prefer one complete file per write over a stub you patch afterwards.\n\nEditor/LSP diagnostics that appear mid-way through a multi-file change are expected, not failures.\nFinish the coherent unit of work, then run `go vet ./...`. Do not abandon the plan to chase a\ndiagnostic caused by an edit you have not finished making.\n\n## Verifying code\n\n- Quick compile check: `go vet ./...`.\n- Tests: `make test` (all tests plus `lint` + `vuln-check`).\n- Integration tests: `make test-integration`.\n- Run the full `make test` only at the end of a big feature, and ask the user first.\n- If you changed `.go` files, at the end of the whole task ask whether to run `make fmt lint`.\n\n## Tools\n\n- Prefer `rg` (ripgrep) over `grep`.\n- Externally run CLI/tool exit status: always capture with `EXIT_CODE=$?` on the line right after the command, then test `$EXIT_CODE`. Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Imports are: `encoding/base64`, `io`, `log/slog`, `net/http`, and `github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web`.\n235: 4. Use a `switch` statement (not if/else ladders) per `branching-logic-flow` skill.\n236: 5. `RawBody` is `*string` with `omitempty`: nil for GET (field absent), non-nil for POST\n237:    (field always present, `\"\"` for an empty body). Take the pointer with `new(expr)` — do not\n238:    add a `ptr` helper.\n239: \n240: ### W2: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp_test.go`\n241: \n242: | Field | Value |\n243: |-------|-------|\n244: | wave | 1 |\n245: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp_test.go` |\n246: | reads | `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp_test.go` (for test patterns: httptest.NewRequest, httptest.NewRecorder, mux.ServeHTTP) |\n247: | depends on | W1 (mirrorapp must exist, may not be compiled yet) |\n248: | done when | `go test ./app/domain/mirrorapp/ -v` passes all cases |\n249: \n250: **do:**\n251: \n252: 1. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Primitives only.\n156: //\n157: // RawBody is a *string so GET can omit the field (nil) while POST with an\n158: // empty body still emits \"raw_body\":\"\" (non-nil pointer to \"\"). A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Use a `switch` statement (not if/else ladders) per `branching-logic-flow` skill.\n236: 5. `RawBody` is `*string` with `omitempty`: nil for GET (field absent), non-nil for POST\n237:    (field always present, `\"\"` for an empty body). Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Primitives only.\n156: //\n157: // RawBody is a *string so GET can omit the field (nil) while POST with an\n158: // empty body still emits \"raw_body\":\"\" (non-nil pointer to \"\"). A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Kept as a guard for\n209: \t\t// direct invocation in tests.\n210: \t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n211: \t\treturn\n212: \t}\n213: }\n214: \n215: // Routes registers the mirror domain routes onto the mux.\n216: func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux) {\n217: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n218: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"POST /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n219: }\n220: \n221: // mirrorHeaders returns a copy of the header map with redacted headers removed.\n222: func mirrorHeaders(h http.Header) map[string][]string {\n223: \tout := make(map[string][]string, len(h))\n224: \tfor k, v := range h {\n225: \t\tif redactedHeaders[k] {\n226: \t\t\tcontinue\n227: \t\t}\n228: \t\tout[k] = v\n229: \t}\n230: \treturn out\n231: }\n232: ```\n233: \n234: 3. Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create directory `app/domain/mirrorapp/`.\n132: 2. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Primitives only.\n156: //\n157: // RawBody is a *string so GET can omit the field (nil) while POST with an\n158: // empty body still emits \"raw_body\":\"\" (non-nil pointer to \"\"). A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Kept as a guard for\n209: \t\t// direct invocation in tests.\n210: \t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n211: \t\treturn\n212: \t}\n213: }\n214: \n215: // Routes registers the mirror domain routes onto the mux.\n216: func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux) {\n217: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"GET /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n218: \tmux.HandleFunc(\"POST /mirror\", a.Mirror)\n219: }\n220: \n221: // mirrorHeaders returns a copy of the header map with redacted headers removed.\n222: func mirrorHeaders(h http.Header) map[string][]string {\n223: \tout := make(map[string][]string, len(h))\n224: \tfor k, v := range h {\n225: \t\tif redactedHeaders[k] {\n226: \t\t\tcontinue\n227: \t\t}\n228: \t\tout[k] = v\n229: \t}\n230: \treturn out\n231: }\n232: ```\n233: \n234: 3. A new route, type, package, or dependency never qualifies.\n- Never reference other plan files in the project unless the user explicitly does.\n\n## Git\n\n- You may stage, commit, and push to feature branches, and open or update PRs and issues.\n- Never merge, never push to the main branch, never force-push, and never delete branches or tags. Every crossing goes through a named converter — never assign across a boundary directly:\n\n- App → Business: `toBus<Type>` (parses + validates, returns `errs.FieldErrors`)\n- Business → App: `fromBus<Type>Response` (strong → primitive, explicit)\n- Business → Storage: `toDB<Type>`\n- Storage → Business: `toBus<Type>` (native → strong, returns error)\n\n## Editing order\n\nEdit so the tree is never gratuitously broken between tool calls:\n\n- Write a type before the code that references it — `model.go` before the handler that uses it.\n- Change an import and its call site in the **same** edit; never add an unused import and fix it later.\n- Prefer one complete file per write over a stub you patch afterwards.\n\nEditor/LSP diagnostics that appear mid-way through a multi-file change are expected, not failures.\nFinish the coherent unit of work, then run `go vet ./...`. Do not abandon the plan to chase a\ndiagnostic caused by an edit you have not finished making.\n\n## Verifying code\n\n- Quick compile check: `go vet ./...`.\n- Tests: `make test` (all tests plus `lint` + `vuln-check`).\n- Integration tests: `make test-integration`.\n- Run the full `make test` only at the end of a big feature, and ask the user first.\n- If you changed `.go` files, at the end of the whole task ask whether to run `make fmt lint`.\n\n## Tools\n\n- Prefer `rg` (ripgrep) over `grep`.\n- Externally run CLI/tool exit status: always capture with `EXIT_CODE=$?` on the line right after the command, then test `$EXIT_CODE`. Execute waves in order. Do not re-derive the design: every path, symbol, and signature you need is below.\n8: \n9: ## Resolved specification\n10: \n11: A single handler `mirrorapp.Mirror` serves both `GET /mirror` and `POST /mirror`.\n12: \n13: ### GET /mirror behavior\n14: \n15: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"GET\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n16: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n17: 3. Build `mirroredResponse` with `RawBody` left `nil` (omitted from JSON by `omitempty`).\n18: 4. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n19: 5. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n20: \n21: ### POST /mirror behavior\n22: \n23: 1. Read `r.Method` (literal `\"POST\"`), `r.URL.Path`, `r.URL.Query()`, `r.Header`.\n24: 2. Exclude redacted headers (below) from the mirror.\n25: 3. If `r.ContentLength > limit`, return 400: `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}`.\n26: 4. Read body bytes with `io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))`.\n27: 5. If read error, return 500: `{\"error\":\"internal error\"}`.\n28: 6. Base64-encode the body bytes and assign the result to `RawBody`. Always assign, even when\n29:    the body is empty — an empty body base64-encodes to `\"\"`, and POST must emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`\n30:    rather than omitting the field. See \"Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\" below.\n31: 7. Serialize via `web.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)`.\n32: 8. Response: 200 `application/json`.\n33: \n34: ### Why `RawBody` is a `*string`\n35: \n36: This is settled. Do not re-derive it, and do not \"simplify\" it back to a plain `string`.\n37: \n38: The two requirements are:\n39: \n40: - GET must **omit** `raw_body` entirely.\n41: - POST with an empty body must **emit** `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n42: \n43: A plain `string` with `omitempty` cannot satisfy both: `omitempty` drops the empty string, so the\n44: POST-empty-body case would lose the field. Dropping `omitempty` cannot satisfy both either: GET\n45: would then emit `\"raw_body\":\"\"`.\n46: \n47: `*string` with `omitempty` satisfies both, because `omitempty` omits a nil pointer but keeps a\n48: non-nil pointer to the empty string:\n49: \n50: | Case | `RawBody` | JSON |\n51: |------|-----------|------|\n52: | GET | `nil` | field absent |\n53: | POST, empty body | `new(\"\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"\"` |\n54: | POST, body `hello` | `new(\"aGVsbG8=\")` | `\"raw_body\":\"aGVsbG8=\"` |\n55: \n56: Use Go 1.26's `new(expr)` to take the pointer — `resp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))`.\n57: Do **not** hand-roll a `ptr[T any]` generic helper; `new(expr)` accepts any expression as of Go 1.26\n58: and a local helper would shadow it.\n59: \n60: ### Limit value\n61: \n62: `const limit = 512 * 1024` (524,288 bytes = 512 KB).\n63: \n64: ### Header redaction\n65: \n66: The following headers are **excluded** from the `Headers` map:\n67: - `Authorization`\n68: - `Cookie`\n69: - `Proxy-Authorization`\n70: \n71: ### JSON output shape\n72: \n73: **GET /mirror** (`raw_body` absent by `omitempty`):\n74: ```json\n75: {\"method\":\"GET\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...}}\n76: ```\n77: \n78: **POST /mirror** (`raw_body` always present, `\"\"` when the body is empty):\n79: ```json\n80: {\"method\":\"POST\",\"path\":\"/mirror\",\"queries\":{...},\"headers\":{...},\"raw_body\":\"base64...\"}\n81: ```\n82: \n83: ### Edge cases\n84: \n85: | Case | Outcome |\n86: |------|---------|\n87: | GET with no query params | `queries: {}` |\n88: | GET with no headers beyond stdlib | Only stdlib headers (e.g., Accept) |\n89: | POST with empty body | `raw_body: \"\"` |\n90: | POST body > 512 KB | 400, `{\"error\":\"request body exceeds 512KB limit\"}` |\n91: | POST with chunked encoding | First 512 KB read, rest discarded by LimitReader |\n92: | POST with malformed UTF-8 | Base64-encoded, no decode attempt |\n93: | POST Content-Length 0 | 200, `raw_body: \"\"` |\n94: | PUT/DELETE on `/mirror` | **405** `Method Not Allowed`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, POST` — see below |\n95: | GET `/mirror/` with trailing slash | 404 (strict mux), body `404 page not found` |\n96: \n97: **On 405 vs 404 for PUT/DELETE.** Routes are registered with Go 1.22+ method patterns\n98: (`\"GET /mirror\"`, `\"POST /mirror\"`). When the *path* matches but the *method* does not,\n99: `http.ServeMux` returns **405** with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, body\n100: `Method Not Allowed\\n`, and an `Allow` header. It does **not** fall through to 404. The `Allow`\n101: value is `GET, HEAD, POST` — `HEAD` is included automatically because a `GET` pattern is\n102: registered. Only a path that matches no pattern at all (such as `/mirror/`) yields 404.\n103: \n104: ## Contracts\n105: \n106: | Symbol | Signature | Status |\n107: |--------|-----------|--------|\n108: | `mirrorapp.App` | `type App struct { log *slog.Logger }` | New |\n109: | `NewApp` | `func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App` | New |\n110: | `Routes` | `func (a *App) Routes(mux *http.ServeMux)` | New |\n111: | `(*App).Mirror` | `func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)` | New |\n112: | `mirroredResponse` | `type mirroredResponse struct { Method string json:\"method\"; Path string json:\"path\"; Queries map[string][]string json:\"queries\"; Headers map[string][]string json:\"headers\"; RawBody *string json:\"raw_body,omitempty\" }` | New |\n113: | `redactedHeaders` | `var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\"Authorization\": true, \"Cookie\": true, \"Proxy-Authorization\": true}` | New |\n114: | `limit` | `const limit = 512 * 1024` | New |\n115: | `mirrorHeaders` | `func mirrorHeaders(http.Header) map[string][]string` | New, unexported helper |\n116: \n117: ## Work units\n118: \n119: ### W1: Create `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go`\n120: \n121: | Field | Value |\n122: |-------|-------|\n123: | wave | 1 |\n124: | owns | `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` |\n125: | reads | `cmd/service/main.go` (for import path, pattern), `foundation/web/respond.go` (for Respond/RespondError signatures), `app/domain/helloapp/helloapp.go` (for App struct + constructor pattern) |\n126: | depends on | — |\n127: | done when | `go vet ./app/domain/mirrorapp/` compiles without error |\n128: \n129: **do:**\n130: \n131: 1. Create file `app/domain/mirrorapp/mirrorapp.go` with the following complete contents:\n133: \n134: ```go\n135: // Package mirrorapp provides the mirror endpoint for echoing requests.\n136: package mirrorapp\n137: \n138: import (\n139: \t\"encoding/base64\"\n140: \t\"io\"\n141: \t\"log/slog\"\n142: \t\"net/http\"\n143: \n144: \t\"github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web\"\n145: )\n146: \n147: const limit = 512 * 1024\n148: \n149: var redactedHeaders = map[string]bool{\n150: \t\"Authorization\":       true,\n151: \t\"Cookie\":              true,\n152: \t\"Proxy-Authorization\": true,\n153: }\n154: \n155: // mirroredResponse carries the mirror output. Primitives only.\n156: //\n157: // RawBody is a *string so GET can omit the field (nil) while POST with an\n158: // empty body still emits \"raw_body\":\"\" (non-nil pointer to \"\"). A plain\n159: // string cannot express both.\n160: type mirroredResponse struct {\n161: \tMethod  string              `json:\"method\"`\n162: \tPath    string              `json:\"path\"`\n163: \tQueries map[string][]string `json:\"queries\"`\n164: \tHeaders map[string][]string `json:\"headers\"`\n165: \tRawBody *string             `json:\"raw_body,omitempty\"`\n166: }\n167: \n168: // App manages the mirror endpoint.\n169: type App struct {\n170: \tlog *slog.Logger\n171: }\n172: \n173: // NewApp constructs a mirror app for use.\n174: func NewApp(log *slog.Logger) *App {\n175: \treturn &App{log: log}\n176: }\n177: \n178: // Mirror echoes back a JSON mirror of the incoming request.\n179: func (a *App) Mirror(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n180: \tresp := mirroredResponse{\n181: \t\tMethod:  r.Method,\n182: \t\tPath:    r.URL.Path,\n183: \t\tQueries: r.URL.Query(),\n184: \t\tHeaders: mirrorHeaders(r.Header),\n185: \t}\n186: \n187: \tswitch r.Method {\n188: \tcase http.MethodGet:\n189: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n190: \t\treturn\n191: \tcase http.MethodPost:\n192: \t\tif r.ContentLength > limit {\n193: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"request body exceeds 512KB limit\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n194: \t\t\treturn\n195: \t\t}\n196: \n197: \t\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(r.Body, limit))\n198: \t\tif err != nil {\n199: \t\t\tweb.RespondError(a.log, w, \"internal error\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n200: \t\t\treturn\n201: \t\t}\n202: \n203: \t\tresp.RawBody = new(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(body))\n204: \t\tweb.Respond(a.log, w, resp, http.StatusOK)\n205: \t\treturn\n206: \tdefault:\n207: \t\t// Unreachable through the registered routes: the mux answers any\n208: \t\t// other method with 405 before the handler runs. Imports are: `encoding/base64`, `io`, `log/slog`, `net/http`, and `github.com/ardanlabs/demo-skills/foundation/web`.\n235: 4.
